Full analysis of the scalar-induced gravitational waves for the curvature perturbation with local-type non-Gaussianities
Chen Yuan, De-Shuang Meng, Qing-Guo Huang

Abstract
Primordial black holes (PBHs) are supposed to form through the gravitational collapse of regions with large density fluctuations. The formation of PBHs inevitably leads to the emission of scalar-induced gravitational wave (SIGW) signals, offering a unique opportunity to test the hypothesis of PBHs as a constituent of dark matter (DM). Previous studies have calculated the energy spectrum of SIGWs in local-type non-Gaussian models, primarily considering the contributions from the F NL F_{\mathrm{NL}} -order or the G NL G_{\mathrm{NL}} -order while neglecting connected diagrams. In this study, we extend the previous work by (i) considering the full contribution of non-Gaussian diagrams up to the G NL G_{\mathrm{NL}} -order; (ii) deriving the generic scaling of the SIGW energy spectrum in the infrared region. We derive semi-analytical results applicable to arbitrary primordial power spectra and numerically evaluate the energy spectrum of SIGWs for a log-normal power spectrum.



I
Introduction

The nature of dark matter (DM) poses a fundamental enigma in astrophysics that has been puzzling for decades. Although its existence can be inferred from its gravitational effects, there remains a significant dearth of knowledge regarding its composition and properties. Among the potential DM candidates, primordial black holes (PBHs) have attracted considerable attention.
PBHs are hypothesized to have formed through the gravitational collapse of regions with over-density during the radiation-dominated epoch immediately after the corresponding perturbation mode entered the horizon
Zel’dovich and Novikov 1967
;
Hawking 1971
;
Carr and Hawking 1974
;
Carr 1975
. And the mass of PBHs is related to the comoving wavelength of the perturbation mode. Numerous studies have been conducted to constrain the abundance of PBHs across a wide mass range
Carr et al. 2010
;
Graham et al. 2015
;
Niikura et al. 2019a
;
Tisserand et al. 2007
;
Niikura et al. 2019b
;
Wang et al. 2018
;
Chen and Huang 2020
;
Brandt 2016
;
Chen et al. 2020
;
Montero-Camacho et al. 2019
;
Laha 2019
;
Dasgupta et al. 2020
;
Laha et al. 2020
;
Saha and Laha 2022
;
Ray et al. 2021
;
Zheng et al. 2023
;
Liu et al. 2023a
;
Chen et al. 2023
;
Chen et al. 2019
;
Mittal et al. 2022
. However, the question of whether PBHs within the mass range of
[10^{-16},10^{-14}]M_{\odot}
and
[10^{-13},10^{-12}]M_{\odot}
could account for the entirety of DM remains unresolved (see e.g.,
Carr et al. 2021
for review of constraints on PBHs).

Non-Gaussianity, characterized by deviations from Gaussian statistics, plays a significant role in the abundance of PBHs by affecting the tail of the probability density function (PDF) of curvature perturbations
Franciolini et al. 2018
;
Biagetti et al. 2018
;
Atal and Germani 2019
;
Passaglia et al. 2019
;
Atal et al. 2019
;
Atal et al. 2020
;
Taoso and Urbano 2021
;
Biagetti et al. 2021
;
Davies et al. 2022
. As a result, PBH formation might be significantly enhanced or suppressed by non-Gaussian effects.

The recent detection of gravitational waves (GWs) from the merger of two black holes by the LIGO-Virgo Collaboration
Abbott et al. 2016
;
Abbott et al. 2021
has inaugurated the era of GW astronomy and sparked renewed interest in the potential role of PBH as constituents of DM
Sasaki et al. 2016
;
Chen and Huang 2018
;
Raidal et al. 2019
;
De Luca et al. 2020a
;
Hall et al. 2020
;
Bhagwat et al. 2021
;
Hütsi et al. 2020
;
Wong et al. 2021
;
De Luca et al. 2021
;
Franciolini et al. 2022
;
Chen et al. 2022
. When the primordial scalar power spectrum experiences amplification on small scales, the quadratic terms of linear scalar perturbations give rise to a second-order tensor mode that can overwhelm the inflationary first-order tensor mode. This second-order tensor mode is known as scalar-induced gravitational waves (SIGWs)
Ananda et al. 2007
;
Baumann et al. 2007
. The SIGWs generated during the formation of PBHs provide a new way to hunt for PBHs
Ananda et al. 2007
;
Baumann et al. 2007
;
Saito and Yokoyama 2009
;
Arroja et al. 2009
;
Assadullahi and Wands 2010
;
Bugaev and Klimai 2010a
;
Bugaev and Klimai 2010b
;
Saito and Yokoyama 2010
;
Bugaev and Klimai 2011
;
Alabidi et al. 2013
;
Nakama and Suyama 2016
;
Nakama et al. 2017
;
Inomata et al. 2017
;
Orlofsky et al. 2017
;
Garcia-Bellido et al. 2017
;
Sasaki et al. 2018
;
Espinosa et al. 2018
;
Kohri and Terada 2018
;
Cai et al. 2019a
;
Bartolo et al. 2019a
;
Bartolo et al. 2019b
;
Unal 2019
;
Byrnes et al. 2019
;
Inomata and Nakama 2019
;
Clesse et al. 2018
;
Cai et al. 2019b
;
Inomata et al. 2019
;
Cai et al. 2019c
;
Yuan et al. 2019
;
Cai et al. 2020
;
Lu et al. 2019
;
Yuan et al. 2020a
;
Tomikawa and Kobayashi 2020
;
De Luca et al. 2020b
;
Yuan et al. 2020b
;
Inomata et al. 2020
;
Yuan and Huang 2021a
;
Papanikolaou et al. 2021
;
Zhang et al. 2020
;
Kapadia et al. 2021
;
Zhang et al. 2021
;
Domènech et al. 2021
;
Dalianis and Kouvaris 2021
;
Atal and Domènech 2021
;
Chen et al. 2022
;
Franciolini 2021
;
Witkowski et al. 2022
;
Balaji et al. 2022
;
Cang et al. 2022
;
Gehrman et al. 2022
;
Braglia et al. 2021
;
Papanikolaou 2022
;
Qiu et al. 2023
;
Escrivà et al. 2022
;
Meng et al. 2023
;
Gehrman et al. 2023
;
Dandoy et al. 2023
;
Ferrante et al. 2023a
;
Afzal et al. 2023
;
Antoniadis et al. 2023
;
Inomata et al. 2023
;
Gu et al. 2023
;
Liu et al. 2023b
;
Yi et al. 2023
;
You et al. 2023
;
Jin et al. 2023
;
Balaji et al. 2023
;
Liu et al. 2023c
;
Basilakos et al. 2023
;
Ragavendra 2022
;
Franciolini et al. 2023
;
Ferrante et al. 2023b
. For reviews of SIGW, see
Yuan and Huang 2021b
;
Domènech 2021
.

Previous studies have considered the local-type non-Gaussianity to the second order (or the
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
-order)
Unal 2019
;
Adshead et al. 2021
, but it is necessary to extend to the third order (or the
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
-order) for a general analysis of GWs induced by non-Gaussian scalar curvature perturbations. While literature
Yuan and Huang 2021a
discusses the contribution to the
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
term, it overlooks the contribution of the connected components, which are not zero. More recently,
Abe et al. 2023
studied the non-Gaussian contribution up to
G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}
order using the diagram approach. However, as we will show in this paper, the full contribution of
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
non-Gaussianities include
G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}
and
G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}
terms which have not been investigated in previous studies.
In this paper, we perform an extensive analysis of GWs induced by local-type non-Gaussian curvature perturbations, encompassing all the contributions up to the
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
-order. We also study the scaling in the infrared region for a generic power spectrum.

II
The energy spectrum of SIGWs

Let’s begin from the FRW perturbated metric in Newton gauge, namely

\mathrm{d}s^{2}=a^{2}\left\{-(1+2\phi)\mathrm{d}\eta^{2}+\left[(1-2\psi)\delta_{ij}+\frac{h_{ij}}{2}\right]\mathrm{d}x^{i}\mathrm{d}x^{j}\right\},

(1)

where
\phi
and
\psi
are the scalar modes,
h_{ij}
is the transverse and traceless second-order tensor mode and
a
is the scale factor. During radiation dominated (RD) period, the stress tensor is described by perfect fluid and
\phi=\psi
in the absence of anisotropies. The equation of motion for
h_{ij}
is given by the second-order perturbative Einstein equation. In momentum space, we have

h_{\lambda,\textbf{k}}^{\prime\prime}(\eta)+2\mathcal{H}h_{\lambda,\textbf{k}}^{\prime}(\eta)+k^{2}h_{\lambda,\textbf{k}}(\eta)=4S_{\lambda,\textbf{k}}(\eta),

(2)

where
\mathcal{H}\equiv a^{\prime}/a
is the conformal Hubble parameter and the prime stands for the derivative with respect to the conformal time
\eta
. The subscript
\lambda
indicates the two different polarization modes of gravitational waves, which are represented by
+
and
\times
.
The source term
S_{\lambda,\textbf{k}}(\eta)
in eq. (
2
) reads
Ananda et al. 2007
;
Baumann et al. 2007

S_{\lambda,\textbf{k}}(\eta)=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q}{(2\pi)^{3}}Q_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})F(q,|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}|,\eta)\Phi_{q}\Phi_{|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}|},

(3)

where
F(p,q,\eta)
is given by

F(p,q,\eta)=3T(p\eta)T(q\eta)+\frac{1}{\mathcal{H}}\left[T^{\prime}(p\eta)T(q\eta)+T(p\eta)T^{\prime}(q\eta)\right]+\frac{1}{\mathcal{H}^{2}}T^{\prime}(p\eta)T^{\prime}(q\eta),

(4)

and
T(k\eta)
is the transfer function, encoding the linear evolution of the scalar mode
\phi_{k}
after re-entering the horizon following the end of inflation and is given by the first-order Einstein equation, namely

\phi_{k}(\eta)\equiv\Phi_{k}T(k\eta)=\Phi_{k}{9\over(k\eta)^{2}}\left({\sin(k\eta/\sqrt{3})\over k\eta/\sqrt{3}}-\cos(k\eta/\sqrt{3})\right),

(5)

where
\Phi_{k}
represents the initial value of
\phi_{k}
when it enters the horizon and is also the value of
\phi_{k}
at the end of inflation because scalar perturbation remains conserved on super-horizon scales. Note that
F(p,q,\eta)
is symmetric for
p
and
q
and unbolded symbols represent the modulus of a vector, and the same convention applies below in this paper. The projection factor
Q_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})
in Eq. (
3
) is defined by

Q_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})\equiv e_{ij}^{\lambda}(\textbf{k})q_{i}q_{j},

(6)

where the polarization tensors are defined as
e_{ij}^{+}=(e_{i}e_{j}-\bar{e}_{i}\bar{e}_{j})/\sqrt{2}
and
e_{ij}^{\times}=(e_{i}\bar{e}_{j}+\bar{e}_{i}e_{j})/\sqrt{2}
and
e(\textbf{k})
and
\bar{e}(\textbf{k})
are a pair of orthogonal basis vectors perpendicular to
k
. It obeys the following symmetries:

Q_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})=Q_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}\pm\mathbf{k})=Q_{\lambda}(-\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})=Q_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{q})=Q_{\lambda}(-\mathbf{k},-\mathbf{q}).

(7)

Here we choose
e=(1,0,0)
,
\bar{e}=(0,1,0)
and
\bm{k}=(0,0,k)
and we can express the vector
q
explicitly as

\mathbf{q}=q(\sin\theta\cos\phi,\sin\theta\sin\phi,\cos\theta).

(8)

Then we can directly write out

Q_{\lambda}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})=\frac{q^{2}}{\sqrt{2}}\sin^{2}\theta\times\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\cos(2\phi),\quad\lambda=+\\
\sin(2\phi),\quad\lambda=\times\end{array}\right..

(9)

Eq. (
2
) can be solved by Green’s function

h_{\lambda,\textbf{k}}(\eta)={4\over a(\eta)}\int_{0}^{\eta}g_{k}(\eta;\eta^{\prime})a(\eta^{\prime})S_{\lambda,\textbf{k}}(\eta)\mathrm{d}\eta^{\prime},

(10)

where the Green’s function takes the form
g_{k}(\eta;\eta^{\prime})={1\over k}\sin(k\eta-k\eta^{\prime})
during RD.

The power spectrum,
P_{\lambda}\left(k,\eta\right)
, and the dimensionless power spectrum of GWs,
\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}\left(k,\eta\right)
, are defined as

\left<h_{\lambda,\textbf{k}}(\eta)h_{\lambda^{\prime},\textbf{k}^{\prime}}(\eta)\right>=(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\right)\delta^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}P_{\lambda}\left(k,\eta\right)=(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}\left(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\right)\delta^{\lambda\lambda^{\prime}}\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}\left(k,\eta\right).

(11)

The energy density of GWs,
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k,\eta)
, is an observed quantity, defined as the energy of GWs per logarithm frequency normalized by the critical energy,
\rho_{c}(\eta)
, takes the form

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k,\eta)\equiv{1\over\rho_{c}}{\mathrm{d}\rho_{\mathrm{GW}}\over\mathrm{d}\ln k}={1\over 48}\left(k\over\mathcal{H}\right)^{2}\sum_{\lambda=+,\times}\overline{\mathcal{P}_{\lambda}\left(k,\eta\right)},

(12)

where the overbar denotes the time average. The density parameter at the matter-radiation equality is
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k)\simeq\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k,k\eta\rightarrow\infty)
and the quality that would be observed today can be obtained by
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW},0}(k)=\Omega_{r}\times\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k)
, where
\Omega_{r}
is the density parameter of radiation today.

Using Eq. (
3
), Eq. (
10
), Eq. (
11
) and Eq. (
12
),
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k)
can be expressed by

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k)=\frac{k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\left(\frac{k}{\mathcal{H}}\right)^{2}\sum_{\lambda=+,\times}

\displaystyle\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}Q_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}\right)Q_{\lambda}\left(\mathbf{k^{\prime}},\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\right)\overline{\tilde{I}\left(q,\left|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}\right|,k\eta\to\infty\right)\tilde{I}\left(q^{\prime},\left|\mathbf{k^{\prime}}-\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\right|,k\eta\to\infty\right)}

(13)

\displaystyle\times\left\langle\left\langle\Phi_{\bm{q}}\Phi_{\bm{k-q}}\Phi_{\bm{q^{\prime}}}\Phi_{\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}}}\right\rangle\right\rangle,

where we define
\left\langle\left\langle\Phi_{\bm{q}}\Phi_{\bm{k-q}}\Phi_{\bm{q^{\prime}}}\Phi_{\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}}}\right\rangle\right\rangle
as the remaining part after extracting
(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}\left(\bm{k}+\bm{k^{\prime}}\right)
from the terms containing
\delta^{3}\left(\bm{k}+\bm{k^{\prime}}\right)
in the four-point function
\left\langle\Phi_{\bm{q}}\Phi_{\bm{k-q}}\Phi_{\bm{q^{\prime}}}\Phi_{\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}}}\right\rangle
, i.e.

\left\langle\Phi_{\bm{q}}\Phi_{\bm{k-q}}\Phi_{\bm{q^{\prime}}}\Phi_{\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}}}\right\rangle\equiv(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}\left(\bm{k}+\bm{k^{\prime}}\right)\left\langle\left\langle\Phi_{\bm{q}}\Phi_{\bm{k-q}}\Phi_{\bm{q^{\prime}}}\Phi_{\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}}}\right\rangle\right\rangle,

(14)

and the kernel function
\tilde{I}(p,q,\eta)
is defined as

\tilde{I}(p,q,\eta)\equiv\int\mathrm{d}\eta^{\prime}{a(\eta^{\prime})\over a(\eta)}g_{k}(\eta;\eta^{\prime})F(p,q,\eta^{\prime}),

(15)

which contains all the time-dependent terms.

By substituting Eq. (
4
), Eq. (
5
), Eq. (
9
) and Eq. (
15
) into Eq. (
13
), applying coordinate transformations
u=q/k
,
v=|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q}|/k
and
u^{\prime}=q^{\prime}/k
,
v^{\prime}=|\mathbf{k}-\mathbf{q^{\prime}}|/k
, and then averaging over time, we obtain
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k)
:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k)=\frac{k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\left\langle\left\langle\zeta_{\bm{q}}\zeta_{\bm{k-q}}\zeta_{\bm{q^{\prime}}}\zeta_{\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}}}\right\rangle\right\rangle,

(16)

where we have transformed the scalar perturbation
\Phi
into the comoving curvature perturbation
\zeta
using the relation
\Phi=(2/3)\zeta
and absorbed the coefficient
16/81
and the remaining projection term after removing
\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})
into the kernel function. The resulting new kernel function can be expressed as

\displaystyle I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)=

\displaystyle\frac{9\left(u^{2}+v^{2}-3\right)\left(u^{\prime 2}+v^{\prime 2}-3\right)}{1024u^{3}u^{\prime 3}v^{3}v^{\prime 3}}\left[4u^{2}-\left(u^{2}-v^{2}+1\right)^{2}\right]\left[4u^{\prime 2}-\left(u^{\prime 2}-v^{\prime 2}+1\right)^{2}\right]

(17)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg\{\left[\left(u^{2}+v^{2}-3\right)\ln\left(\left|\frac{(u-v)^{2}-3}{(u+v)^{2}-3}\right|\right)+4uv\right]\left[\left(u^{\prime 2}+v^{\prime 2}-3\right)\ln\left(\left|\frac{(u^{\prime}-v^{\prime})^{2}-3}{(u^{\prime}+v^{\prime})^{2}-3}\right|\right)+4u^{\prime}v^{\prime}\right]

\displaystyle+\pi^{2}\left(u^{2}+v^{2}-3\right)\left(u^{\prime 2}+v^{\prime 2}-3\right)\Theta\left(u+v-\sqrt{3}\right)\Theta\left(u^{\prime}+v^{\prime}-\sqrt{3}\right)\Bigg\},

where
\Theta
is the Heaviside function.
It is worth noting that in Eq. (
16
), we have retained the momentum dependence in the integral variable and four-point function without making a change of variables. This is for the convenience of future calculations when dealing with non-Gaussianity. The following relations will be frequently used in subsequent calculations. The transformation relation for the integral variables is as follows:

\int\mathrm{d}^{3}q\to\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi uvk^{3},

(18)

and
\cos\theta
and
\sin\theta
can be expressed by

\cos\theta=\frac{1+u^{2}-v^{2}}{2u},\quad\sin\theta=\sqrt{1-\frac{(1+u^{2}-v^{2})^{2}}{4u^{2}}}.

(19)

III
GWs induced by local-type non-Gaussian curvature perturbations

The local-type non-Gaussian curvature perturbation
\zeta
is expanded in terms of the Gaussian part
\zeta_{g}
in real space as

\zeta\left(\zeta_{g}\right)=\zeta_{g}+F_{\mathrm{NL}}\left(\zeta_{g}^{2}-\left\langle\zeta_{g}^{2}\right\rangle\right)+G_{\mathrm{NL}}\zeta_{g}^{3},

(20)

where
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
are the dimensionless non-Gaussian parameters, related to the commonly used notations
f_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
g_{\mathrm{NL}}
by
F_{\mathrm{NL}}\equiv 3/5f_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
G_{\mathrm{NL}}\equiv 9/25g_{\mathrm{NL}}
respectively. In momentum space, the curvature perturbation is expanded by convolution of the Gaussian part

\zeta_{\bm{k}}=\zeta_{g}(\bm{k})+F_{\mathrm{NL}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}\zeta_{g}(\bm{p})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-p})+G_{\mathrm{NL}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-p_{1}-p_{2}}).

(21)

Note that we neglect the Fourier transform of the constant term
F_{\mathrm{NL}}\left\langle\zeta_{g}^{2}\right\rangle
since this term leads to
\delta(\bm{k})
and does not contribute to the SIGW power spectrum in the following calculation.
The power spectrum
P_{g}(k)
and the dimensionless power spectrum
\mathcal{P}_{g}(k)
of the Gaussian part curvature perturbation are defined as

\left\langle\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{k}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{k}^{\prime}\right)\right\rangle=(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}\left(\bm{k}+\bm{k}^{\prime}\right)P_{g}\left(k\right)=(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{3}\left(\bm{k}+\bm{k}^{\prime}\right)\frac{2\pi^{2}}{k^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(k\right).

(22)

The influence of non-Gaussianity in curvature perturbations on the GWs spectrum is manifested in the four-point function in Eq. (
16
). By substituting Eq. (
21
) into Eq. (
16
) and employing Wick’s theorem, we can obtain the total GWs energy density spectrum up to the
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
order in local non-Gaussian expansion. Next, we will decompose the GWs spectrum into different powers of
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
.

III.1
Gaussian part

The leading order is the Gaussian part, in which case we have

\displaystyle\left\langle\zeta_{\bm{q}}\zeta_{\bm{k-q}}\zeta_{\bm{q^{\prime}}}\zeta_{\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}}}\right\rangle_{g}=

\displaystyle\left\langle\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{q}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\right)\right\rangle\left\langle\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{k-q}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}}\right)\right\rangle+\left\langle\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{q}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}}\right)\right\rangle\left\langle\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{k-q}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{q}^{\prime}\right)\right\rangle

(23)

\displaystyle+\left\langle\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{q}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{k-q}\right)\right\rangle\left\langle\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{q^{\prime}}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\mathbf{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}}\right)\right\rangle,

where the third term on the right-hand side of the above equation is zero because it corresponds to a disconnected diagram that does not contribute to the physical mechanism, and it also does not contain the
\delta^{3}\left(\bm{k}+\bm{k^{\prime}}\right)
term. Due to symmetry, the contributions of the first two terms on the right-hand side of the above equation are equal. By substituting this equation into Eq. (
16
), we can obtain the Gaussian part of the
GWs spectrum

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q}{(2\pi)^{3}}I^{2}\left(u,v\right)P_{g}\left(q\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q}|\right)\times 2

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}vI^{2}(u,v)\frac{1}{u^{2}v^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(vk\right).

(24)

III.2
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}
terms

Since
\zeta_{g}
is Gaussian curvature perturbation, the odd-order n-point correlation functions of which are zero. Therefore, the GWs spectrum containing
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}
terms is also zero. Therefore, we only need to consider terms containing
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}
and
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}
.

For terms containing
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}
, considering symmetry, the GWs spectrum can be expressed in the following form:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}

(25)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[2\left\langle\left\langle\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q-p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-q-p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q^{\prime}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\right\rangle\right\rangle

\displaystyle+4\left\langle\left\langle\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q-p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-q})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\right\rangle\right\rangle\Bigg].

Performing Wick contraction on the above two six-point functions, there are three distinct non-zero contractions denoted as ’hybrid’ term, ’Z’ term, and ’C’ term as named in ref.
Unal 2019
;
Adshead et al. 2021
. Then we have

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k)=\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{hybrid}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{C}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{Z}(k).

(26)

Note that the ’hybrid’ term is a disconnected term, the ’C’ term and the ’Z’ term are connected terms. Ref.
Yuan and Huang 2021a
omitted all disconnected items. We now demonstrate each of these three parts in detail.

For the ’hybrid’ term, one example of the contraction is shown as follows:

\left\langle\hbox to4.38pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.1875pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}

{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 10.3 L 0 22.83 L 0 22.83 L 0 10.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\vbox to19.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle,

(27)

which is commonly referred to as a disconnected diagram and the term
\delta^{3}(\bm{q}+\bm{q^{\prime}})
is present. According to symmetry, there are two other contractions that yield the same result. Therefore, we need to multiply by a symmetry factor, which in this case is 2. The calculation of a disconnected diagram is relatively straightforward, because in this case
\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})=1
and thus disappears in the integral. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{hybrid}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q}{(2\pi)^{3}}I^{2}\left(u,v\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}}{(2\pi)^{3}}4P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q}|\right)\times 2

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{3}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}I^{2}(u,v)\frac{1}{u^{2}v^{2}u_{1}^{2}v_{1}^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(vk\right),

(28)

where the second equality in the above equation is obtained by performing the coordinate transformation
u_{1}=p_{1}/q
and
v_{1}=|\bm{q-p_{1}}|/q
.

{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 10.3 L 0 18.68 L 0 18.68 L 0 10.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\vbox to19.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle,

(29)

which is commonly referred to as a connected diagram and the term
\delta^{3}(\bm{q}+\bm{q^{\prime}})
is not satisfied. The symmetry factor in this case is 4. Then the calculation will be more complicated than the disconnected diagram because
\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})
will be retained in the integral. In this case, we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{Z}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)4P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q^{\prime}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q-q^{\prime}}|\right)\times 4

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{3\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi^{\prime}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}}{v^{3}v^{\prime 3}w_{012}^{3}}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(vk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v^{\prime}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{012}k\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{3\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}}{v^{3}v^{\prime 3}w_{012}^{3}}

(30)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(vk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v^{\prime}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{012}k\right),

where the third equality is obtained by performing a coordinate transformation
\varphi_{1}=\phi-\phi^{\prime}
and
\varphi_{2}=\phi+\phi^{\prime}
and then we have

\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi^{\prime}\to\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{4\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}=2\pi\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1},

(31)

as
\varphi_{2}
does not appear in the integral.

Additionally,
w_{012}
is defined as follows:

w_{012}^{2}=\frac{|\bm{k-q-q^{\prime}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}=1+u^{2}+u^{\prime 2}+2uu^{\prime}(\sin\theta\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\varphi_{1}+\cos\theta\cos\theta^{\prime})-2u\cos\theta-2u^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime},

(32)

where we have used the following relations

\displaystyle\bm{q}\cdot\bm{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=uu^{\prime}k^{2}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta^{\prime}(\cos\phi\cos\phi^{\prime}+\sin\phi\sin\phi^{\prime})+\cos\theta\cos\theta^{\prime}\right],

(33)

\displaystyle\bm{k}\cdot\bm{q}

\displaystyle=uk^{2}\cos\theta,

(34)

\displaystyle\bm{k}\cdot\bm{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=u^{\prime}k^{2}\cos\theta^{\prime},

(35)

and Eq. (
19
) to replace
\sin
and
\cos
.

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\vbox to16.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle,

(36)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 8. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{C}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)2P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-q^{\prime}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q^{\prime}}|\right)P_{g}\left(q^{\prime}\right)\times 8

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{3\pi}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}}{u^{\prime 3}v^{\prime 3}w_{12}^{3}}

(37)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u^{\prime}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v^{\prime}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{12}k\right),

where

w_{12}^{2}=\frac{|\bm{q-q^{\prime}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}=u^{2}+u^{\prime 2}-2uu^{\prime}(\sin\theta\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\varphi_{1}+\cos\theta\cos\theta^{\prime}).

(38)

III.3
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}
terms

For terms containing
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}
, the GWs spectrum can be expressed in the following form:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{4}}{(2\pi)^{12}}

(39)

\displaystyle\times\left\langle\left\langle\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q-p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-q-p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{3}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{3}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}-p_{4}})\right\rangle\right\rangle.

Performing Wick contraction on the above eight-point function, there are three distinct non-zero contractions denoted as ’reducible’ term, ’planar’ term, and ’non-planar’ term as also named in ref.
Garcia-Bellido et al. 2017
;
Adshead et al. 2021
. Then we can write

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}(k)=\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{re}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{planar}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{np}(k).

(40)

We now demonstrate each of these three parts in detail.

For the ’reducible’ term, one example of the contraction is shown as follows:

\left\langle\hbox to4.38pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.1875pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{}{}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.1875pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\hbox to4.38pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.1875pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.1875pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{q-p_{1}})\hbox to4.38pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.1875pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.1875pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

(41)

which is a disconnected diagram and the symmetry factor is 8. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{re}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q}{(2\pi)^{3}}I^{2}\left(u,v\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q-p_{2}}|\right)\times 8

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}{3}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}I^{2}(u,v)\frac{1}{u^{2}v^{2}u_{1}^{2}v_{1}^{2}u_{2}^{2}v_{2}^{2}}

(42)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}vk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v_{2}vk\right),

where the second equality in the above equation is obtained by performing the coordinate transformation
u_{1}=p_{1}/q
,
v_{1}=|\bm{q-p_{1}}|/q
and
u_{2}=p_{2}/|\bm{k-q}|
,
v_{2}=|\bm{k-q-p_{2}}|/|\bm{k-q}|
.

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.1875pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}

{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 10.3 L 0 22.83 L 0 22.83 L 0 10.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}-p_{4}})\vbox to19.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle,

(43)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 32. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{planar}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}}{(2\pi)^{3}}P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)

\displaystyle\times P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{1}}|\right)\times 32

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}{6\pi^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi^{\prime}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\phi_{1}

\displaystyle\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}}{(u_{1}v_{1}w_{13}w_{23})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{23}k\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}{3\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}

(44)

\displaystyle\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}}{(u_{1}v_{1}w_{13}w_{23})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{23}k\right),

where the third equality in the above equation is obtained by performing a coordinate transformation
\varphi_{1}=\phi-\phi^{\prime}
,
\varphi_{2}=\phi-\phi_{1}
, and
\varphi_{3}=\phi+\phi^{\prime}
with the Jacobi 2 and then
\varphi_{3}
can be integrated out. Additionally,
w_{13}
and
w_{23}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{13}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{|\bm{q-p_{1}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}=u^{2}+u_{1}^{2}-2uu_{1}(\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1}),

(45)

\displaystyle w_{23}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{1}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}=u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}-2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right].

(46)

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 10.3 L 0 26.98 L 0 26.98 L 0 10.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}-p_{4}})\vbox to22.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle,

(47)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 16. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{np}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}}{(2\pi)^{3}}

\displaystyle\times P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k+p_{1}-q-q^{\prime}}|\right)\times 16

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}{6\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}

(48)

\displaystyle\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}}{(u_{1}w_{13}w_{23}w_{0123})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{23}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{0123}k\right),

where
w_{0123}
is defined as

\displaystyle w_{0123}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k+p_{1}-q-q^{\prime}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+u^{2}+u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}-2u\cos\theta-2u^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime}+2u_{1}\cos\theta_{1}+2uu^{\prime}\left(\sin\theta\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\varphi_{1}+\cos\theta\cos\theta^{\prime}\right)

(49)

\displaystyle-2uu_{1}\left(\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1}\right)-2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right].

III.4
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
terms

For terms containing
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
, we can easily obtain that

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{G_{\mathrm{NL}}}(k)=12G_{\mathrm{NL}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}P_{g}(p)\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k)=12G_{\mathrm{NL}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p}{p}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p)\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k).

(50)

In particular, we normalize the power spectrum
\mathcal{P}_{g}(p)
to be

A=\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p}{p}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p),

(51)

then
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{G_{\mathrm{NL}}}(k)=12AG_{\mathrm{NL}}\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k)
holds, where
A
represents the variance of the Gaussian part of the dimensionless curvature perturbation spectrum
\mathcal{P}_{g}(p)
.

III.5
G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}
terms

For terms containing
G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}
, considering symmetry, the GWs spectrum can be expressed in the following form:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{4}}{(2\pi)^{12}}

(52)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[2\left\langle\left\langle\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{3}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-q-p_{3}-p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q^{\prime}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\right\rangle\right\rangle

\displaystyle+4\left\langle\left\langle\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-q})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{3}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{3}-p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\right\rangle\right\rangle\Bigg].

Performing Wick contraction on the above eight-point function, there are four distinct non-zero contractions and we name them as ’2loop’ term, ’tri’ term, ’ring1’ term, and ’ring2’ term. Then we have

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k)=\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{2loop}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring1}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring2}(k).

(53)

We now demonstrate each of these four parts in detail.

The ‘2loop’ term can be easily obtained as

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{2loop}(k)=54G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}P_{g}(p_{1})P_{g}(p_{2})\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k)=54G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{1}\mathrm{d}p_{2}}{p_{1}p_{2}}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{1})\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{2})\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k),

(54)

and for power spectrum satisfying Eq. (
51
), we have
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{2loop}(k)=54A^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k)
.

For the ’tri’ term, one example of the contraction is shown as follows:

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\vbox to22.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle,

(55)

which is a disconnected diagram and the symmetry factor in this case is 6. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q}{(2\pi)^{3}}I^{2}\left(u,v\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}4P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q}|\right)\times 6

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}I^{2}(u,v)\frac{1}{u^{2}v^{2}u_{1}^{2}v_{1}^{2}u_{2}^{2}v_{2}^{2}}

(56)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}v_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v_{2}v_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(vk\right),

where the second equality in the above equation is obtained by performing the coordinate transformation
u_{1}=p_{1}/q
,
v_{1}=|\bm{q-p_{1}}|/q
and
u_{2}=p_{2}/|\bm{q-p_{1}}|
,
v_{2}=|\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}}|/|\bm{q-p_{1}}|
.

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\vbox to22.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle,

(57)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 36. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring1}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}2P_{g}\left(p\right)P_{g}\left(q^{\prime}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q^{\prime}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q+q^{\prime}+p}|\right)\times 36

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}

(58)

\displaystyle\times\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}}{(u^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}w_{123})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u^{\prime}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v^{\prime}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{123}k\right),

where we perform the coordinate transformation
\varphi_{1}=\phi-\phi^{\prime}
,
\varphi_{2}=\phi-\phi_{1}
, and
\varphi_{3}=\phi+\phi^{\prime}
. Additionally,
w_{123}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{123}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{q+q^{\prime}+p}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u^{2}+u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}+2uu^{\prime}\left(\sin\theta\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\varphi_{1}+\cos\theta\cos\theta^{\prime}\right)+2uu_{1}(\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1})

(59)

\displaystyle+2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right],

where
\sin
and
\cos
are replaced using Eq. (
19
).

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\vbox to22.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle,

(60)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 18. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring2}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}

\displaystyle\times 4P_{g}\left(p\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q^{\prime}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k+p-q-q^{\prime}}|\right)\times 18

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}

(61)

\displaystyle\times\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}}{(u_{1}vv^{\prime}w_{0123})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(vk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v^{\prime}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{0123}k\right),

where
w_{0123}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{0123}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k+p-q-q^{\prime}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+u^{2}+u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}+2u_{1}\cos\theta_{1}-2u\cos\theta-2u^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime}+2uu^{\prime}\left(\sin\theta\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\varphi_{1}+\cos\theta\cos\theta^{\prime}\right)

(62)

\displaystyle-2uu_{1}(\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1})-2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right].

III.6
G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}
terms

For terms containing
G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}
, considering symmetry, the GWs spectrum can be expressed in the following form:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{4}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{5}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{6}}{(2\pi)^{18}}4\langle\langle\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{2}})

\displaystyle\times\zeta_{g}(\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{3}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-q-p_{3}-p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{5}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{6}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{5}-p_{6}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\rangle\rangle.

Performing Wick contraction on the above ten-point function, there are four distinct non-zero contractions and we name them as the ‘1loop’ term, the ‘3loop’ term and the ‘ring3’ term. Then we have

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}}(k)=\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{1loop}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{3loop}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring3}(k),

(64)

and we can easily obtain

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{1loop}(k)=

\displaystyle 6G_{\mathrm{NL}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}P_{g}(p)\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring1}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring2}(k)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 6G_{\mathrm{NL}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p}{p}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p)\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring1}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring2}(k)\right),

(65)

and for power spectrum satisfying Eq. (
51
), we have
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{1loop}(k)=6AG_{\mathrm{NL}}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring1}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring2}(k)\right)
.

While the ’3loop’ term can be expressed as

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{3loop}(k)=

\displaystyle 108G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}}{(2\pi)^{9}}P_{g}(p_{1})P_{g}(p_{2})P_{g}(p_{3})\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 108G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{1}\mathrm{d}p_{2}\mathrm{d}p_{3}}{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{1})\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{2})\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{3})\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k),

(66)

and for power spectrum satisfying Eq. (
51
), we have
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{3loop}(k)=108A^{3}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k)
.

The ‘ring3’ term is a disconnected diagram and one example of the contraction is shown as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.1875pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 10.3 L 0 31.13 L 0 31.13 L 0 10.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\vbox to25.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle,

(67)

and the symmetry factor in this contraction is 216. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring3}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}4\times 216

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q^{\prime}}|\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{9G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}}{4\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}

(68)

\displaystyle\times\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}}{(u_{1}u_{2}w_{134}w_{234}v^{\prime})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{134}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{234}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v^{\prime}k\right),

where we perform the coordinate transformation
\varphi_{1}=\phi-\phi^{\prime}
,
\varphi_{2}=\phi-\phi_{1}
,
\varphi_{3}=\phi-\phi_{2}
,
\varphi_{4}=\phi+\phi_{2}
and
\varphi_{4}
can be integrated out to get
2\pi
.
Additionally,
w_{134}
and
w_{234}
are defined as

\displaystyle w_{134}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u^{2}+u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}-2uu_{1}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1}\right]-2uu_{2}(\sin\theta\sin\theta_{2}\cos\varphi_{3}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{2})

(69)

\displaystyle+2u_{1}u_{2}(\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}),

\displaystyle w_{234}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{1}-p_{2}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}-2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right]-2u^{\prime}u_{2}(\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{2})

(70)

\displaystyle+2u_{1}u_{2}(\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}).

III.7
G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}
terms

For terms containing
G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}
, considering symmetry, the GWs spectrum can be expressed in the following form:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{~d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2\left(\phi-\phi^{\prime}\right)I(u,v)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{~d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{~d}^{3}p_{3}\mathrm{~d}^{3}p_{4}\mathrm{~d}^{3}p_{5}\mathrm{~d}^{3}p_{6}\mathrm{~d}^{3}p_{7}\mathrm{~d}^{3}p_{8}}{(2\pi)^{24}}

(71)

\displaystyle\times\left\langle\left\langle\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{p}_{\mathbf{1}}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{p}_{\mathbf{2}}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{q}-\bm{p}_{\mathbf{1}}-\bm{p}_{\mathbf{2}}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{p}_{3}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{p}_{\mathbf{4}}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{k}-\bm{q}-\bm{p}_{\mathbf{3}}-\bm{p}_{\mathbf{4}}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{p}_{\mathbf{5}}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{p}_{\mathbf{6}}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{q}^{\prime}-\bm{p}_{\mathbf{5}}-\bm{p}_{\mathbf{6}}\right)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{p}_{\bm{7}}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{p}_{\bm{8}}\right)\zeta_{g}\left(\bm{k}^{\prime}-\bm{q}^{\prime}-\bm{p}_{\bm{7}}-\bm{p}_{\bm{8}}\right)\right\rangle\right\rangle

Performing Wick contraction on the above twelve-point function, there are 8 distinct non-zero contractions and we name them as the ‘2loops’ term, the ‘4loop’ term, the ‘ring3loop’ term, the ‘double’ term, the ‘bubble’ term, the ‘sand clock’(sc) term, the ‘2rings’ term and the ‘net’ term. Then we have

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}(k)=\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{2loops}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{4loop}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring3loop}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{double}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{bubble}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{sc}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{2rings}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{net}(k),

(72)

and we now demonstrate each of these 8 parts in detail.

We can easily obtain

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{2loops}(k)=

\displaystyle 9G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}P_{g}(p_{1})P_{g}(p_{2})\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring1}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring2}(k)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 9G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{1}\mathrm{d}p_{2}}{p_{1}p_{2}}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{1})\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{2})\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring1}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring2}(k)\right),

(73)

and for power spectrum satisfying Eq. (
51
), we have
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{2loops}(k)=9A^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring1}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring2}(k)\right)
.

The ‘4loop’ term can also be easily obtained as

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{4loop}(k)=81G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{1}\mathrm{d}p_{2}\mathrm{d}p_{3}\mathrm{d}p_{4}}{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}p_{4}}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{1})\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{2})\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{3})\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{4})\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k),

(74)

and for power spectrum satisfying Eq. (
51
), we have
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{4loop}(k)=81A^{4}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{g}(k)
.

Similarly, the ‘ring3loop’ term can be easily obtained as

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring3loop}(k)=3AG_{\mathrm{NL}}\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{ring3}(k)

(75)

The ‘double’ term is a disconnected diagram and one example of the contraction is shown as follows:

\left\langle\hbox to4.38pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.1875pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.1875pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\hbox to4.38pt{\vbox to8.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 2.1875pt\lower-4.44444pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.1875pt}{-2.5pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{68}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

(76)

where
\bm{p_{9}}\equiv\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}}
,
\bm{p_{10}}\equiv\bm{k-q-p_{3}-p_{4}}
,
\bm{p_{11}}\equiv\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{5}-p_{6}}
and
\bm{p_{12}}\equiv\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}-p_{7}-p_{8}}
.
The symmetry factor in this case is 72. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{double}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q}{(2\pi)^{3}}I^{2}\left(u,v\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{4}}{(2\pi)^{12}}P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)

\displaystyle\times P_{g}\left(p_{3}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{4}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q-p_{3}-p_{4}}|\right)\times 72

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}{4}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{3}\int_{|1-u_{3}|}^{1+u_{3}}\mathrm{d}v_{3}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{4}\int_{|1-u_{4}|}^{1+u_{4}}\mathrm{d}v_{4}

\displaystyle\times I^{2}(u,v)\frac{1}{u^{2}v^{2}u_{1}^{2}v_{1}^{2}u_{2}^{2}v_{2}^{2}u_{3}^{2}v_{3}^{2}u_{4}^{2}v_{4}^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}v_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v_{2}v_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{3}vk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{4}v_{3}vk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v_{4}v_{3}vk\right),

where the second equality in the above equation is obtained by performing the coordinate transformation
u_{1}=p_{1}/q
,
v_{1}=|\bm{q-p_{1}}|/q
,
u_{2}=p_{2}/|\bm{q-p_{1}}|
,
v_{2}=|\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}}|/|\bm{q-p_{1}}|
,
u_{3}=p_{3}/|\bm{k-q}|
,
v_{3}=|\bm{k-q-p_{3}}|/|\bm{k-q}|
,
u_{4}=p_{4}/|\bm{k-q-p_{3}}|
,
v_{4}=|\bm{k-q-p_{3}-p_{4}}|/|\bm{k-q-p_{3}}|
.

{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 10.3 L 0 31.13 L 0 31.13 L 0 10.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{p_{12}})\vbox to25.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle,

(78)

where
\bm{p_{9}}
,
\bm{p_{10}}
,
\bm{p_{11}}
and
\bm{p_{12}}
are defined the same as above and the symmetry factor in this case is 648. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{bubble}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}}{(2\pi)^{9}}

\displaystyle\times P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{3}\right)P_{g}\left(\bm{k-p_{1}-p_{2}-p_{3}}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)\times 648

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{27G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}{64\pi^{4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{3}\int_{|1-u_{3}|}^{1+u_{3}}\mathrm{d}v_{3}

(79)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{4}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}u_{3}v_{3}}{(u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}w_{0345}w_{134}w_{234})^{3}}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{3}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{0345}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{134}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{234}k\right),

where we perform the coordinate transformation
\varphi_{1}=\phi-\phi^{\prime}
,
\varphi_{2}=\phi-\phi_{1}
,
\varphi_{3}=\phi-\phi_{2}
,
\varphi_{4}=\phi-\phi_{3}
, and
\varphi_{5}=\phi+\phi^{\prime}
. Additionally,
w_{0345}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{0345}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k-p_{1}-p_{2}-p_{3}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}+u_{3}^{2}-2u_{1}\cos\theta_{1}-2u_{2}\cos\theta_{2}-2u_{3}\cos\theta_{3}+2u_{1}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right]

(80)

\displaystyle+2u_{1}u_{3}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{3}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{4})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{3}\right]+2u_{2}u_{3}\left[\sin\theta_{2}\sin\theta_{3}\cos(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{4})+\cos\theta_{2}\cos\theta_{3}\right]

(81)

where
\bm{p_{9}}
,
\bm{p_{10}}
,
\bm{p_{11}}
and
\bm{p_{12}}
are defined the same as above and the symmetry factor in this case is 648. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{sc}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}}{(2\pi)^{9}}

\displaystyle\times P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{3}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(\bm{k-q-q^{\prime}+p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3}}\right)\times 648

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{27G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}{64\pi^{4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{3}\int_{|1-u_{3}|}^{1+u_{3}}\mathrm{d}v_{3}

(82)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{4}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}u_{3}v_{3}}{(u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}w_{134}w_{234}w)^{3}}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{3}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{134}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{234}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(wk\right),

where we perform the coordinate transformation the same as above. Additionally,
w
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle w^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k-q-q^{\prime}+p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+u^{2}+u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}+u_{3}^{2}-2u\cos\theta-2u^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime}+2u_{1}\cos\theta_{1}+2u_{2}\cos\theta_{2}-2u_{3}\cos\theta_{3}

(83)

\displaystyle+2uu^{\prime}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\varphi_{1}+\cos\theta\cos\theta^{\prime}\right]-2uu_{1}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1}\right]-2uu_{2}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{2}\cos\varphi_{3}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2uu_{3}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{3}\cos\varphi_{4}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{3}\right]-2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right]

\displaystyle-2u^{\prime}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{2}\right]+2u^{\prime}u_{3}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{3}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{4})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{3}\right]

\displaystyle+2u_{1}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right]-2u_{1}u_{3}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{3}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{4})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{3}\right]

\displaystyle-2u_{2}u_{3}\left[\sin\theta_{2}\sin\theta_{3}\cos(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{4})+\cos\theta_{2}\cos\theta_{3}\right].

(84)

where
\bm{p_{9}}
,
\bm{p_{10}}
,
\bm{p_{11}}
and
\bm{p_{12}}
are defined the same as above and the symmetry factor in this case is 648. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{2rings}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}}{(2\pi)^{9}}

\displaystyle\times P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{3}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q+p_{1}+p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(\bm{k-q-q^{\prime}+p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3}}\right)\times 648

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{27G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}{64\pi^{4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{3}\int_{|1-u_{3}|}^{1+u_{3}}\mathrm{d}v_{3}

(85)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{4}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}u_{3}v_{3}}{(u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}w_{134}w_{0134}w)^{3}}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{3}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{134}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{0134}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(wk\right),

where we perform the coordinate transformation the same as above. Additionally,
w_{0134}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{0134}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k-q+p_{1}+p_{2}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+u^{2}+u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}-2u\cos\theta+2u_{1}\cos\theta_{1}+2u_{2}\cos\theta_{2}-2uu_{1}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1}\right]

(86)

\displaystyle-2uu_{2}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{2}\cos\varphi_{3}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{2}\right]+2u_{1}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right].

{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}

{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{p_{12}})\vbox to28.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle,

(87)

where
\bm{p_{9}}
,
\bm{p_{10}}
,
\bm{p_{11}}
and
\bm{p_{12}}
are defined the same as above and the symmetry factor in this case is 1296. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{net}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}}{(2\pi)^{9}}

\displaystyle\times P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{3}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{2}-p_{3}}|\right)P_{g}\left(\bm{k-q+p_{1}-p_{3}}\right)\times 1296

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{27G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}}{32\pi^{4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{3}\int_{|1-u_{3}|}^{1+u_{3}}\mathrm{d}v_{3}

(88)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{4}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}u_{3}v_{3}}{(u_{1}u_{2}u_{3}w_{134}w_{245}w_{0135})^{3}}

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{3}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{134}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{245}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{0135}k\right),

where we perform the coordinate transformation the same as above. Additionally,
w_{245}
and
w_{0135}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{245}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{2}-p_{3}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u^{\prime 2}+u_{2}^{2}+u_{3}^{2}-2u^{\prime}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{2}\right]-2u^{\prime}u_{3}(\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{3}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{4})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{3})

(89)

\displaystyle+2u_{2}u_{3}(\sin\theta_{2}\sin\theta_{3}\cos(\varphi_{3}-\varphi_{4})+\cos\theta_{2}\cos\theta_{3}),

\displaystyle w_{0135}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k-q+p_{1}-p_{3}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+u^{2}+u_{1}^{2}+u_{3}^{2}-2u\cos\theta+2u_{1}\cos\theta_{1}-2u_{3}\cos\theta_{3}-2uu_{1}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1}\right]

(90)

\displaystyle+2uu_{3}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{3}\cos\varphi_{4}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{3}\right]-2u_{1}u_{3}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{3}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{4})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{3}\right].

III.8
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}
terms

For terms containing
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}
, considering symmetry, the GWs spectrum can be expressed in the following form:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{4}}{(2\pi)^{12}}

(91)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[8\left\langle\left\langle\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q-p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{3}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-q-p_{2}-p_{3}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\right\rangle\right\rangle

\displaystyle+4\left\langle\left\langle\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q-p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-q-p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{3}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{3}-p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}})\right\rangle\right\rangle\Bigg].

Performing Wick contraction on the eight-point function, there are four distinct non-zero contractions and we name them as the ’loop’ term, the ’
F^{2}G(1)
’ term, the ’
F^{2}G(2)
’ term, and the ’
F^{2}G(3)
’ term. Then we have

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}}(k)=\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{loop}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G(1)}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G(2)}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G(3)}(k),

(92)

and we now demonstrate each of these four parts in detail.

The ’loop’ term can be easily obtained as

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{loop}(k)=6G_{\mathrm{NL}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p}{p}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p)\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k),

(93)

and for power spectrum satisfying Eq. (
51
), we have
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{loop}(k)=6AG_{\mathrm{NL}}\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k)
.

One example of the contraction of the ’
F^{2}G(1)
’ term is shown as follows:
