(94)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 24. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G(1)}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}4P_{g}\left(p\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-q^{\prime}}|\right)P_{g}\left(\bm{k-q^{\prime}}\right)\times 24

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}}{\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})

(95)

\displaystyle\times\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}}{(u_{1}v^{\prime}w_{13}w_{12})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{12}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v^{\prime}k\right),

where
w_{12}
and
w_{13}
are defined the same as Eq. (
38
) and Eq. (
45
).

(96)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 12. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G(2)}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}8P_{g}\left(p\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q^{\prime}-p}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q^{\prime}}|\right)

\displaystyle\times P_{g}\left(\bm{k-q-q^{\prime}}\right)\times 12

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}}{\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})

(97)

\displaystyle\times\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}}{(u_{1}v^{\prime}w_{23}w_{012})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v^{\prime}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{23}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{012}k\right),

where
w_{23}
and
w_{012}
are defined the same as Eq. (
46
) and Eq. (
32
).

and the symmetry factor in this case is 24. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G(3)}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}8P_{g}\left(p\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q^{\prime}}|\right)

\displaystyle\times P_{g}\left(\bm{q-q^{\prime}-p}\right)\times 24

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}}{\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})

(99)

\displaystyle\times\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}}{(u_{1}v^{\prime}w_{13}w_{123})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v^{\prime}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{123}k\right),

where
w_{13}
is defined the same as Eq. (
45
) and
w_{123}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{123}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{q-q^{\prime}-p}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u^{2}+u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}-2uu^{\prime}\left(\sin\theta\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\varphi_{1}+\cos\theta\cos\theta^{\prime}\right)-2uu_{1}(\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1})

(100)

\displaystyle+2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right].

III.9
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}
terms

For terms containing
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}
, considering symmetry, the GWs spectrum can be expressed in the following form:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{4}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{5}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{6}}{(2\pi)^{18}}

(101)

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[4\langle\langle\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q-p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{3}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-q-p_{2}-p_{3}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{5}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{6}})

\displaystyle\quad\times\zeta_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}-p_{5}-p_{6}})\rangle\rangle+2\langle\langle\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{q-p_{1}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k-q-p_{2}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{3}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{4}})

\displaystyle\quad\times\zeta_{g}(\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{3}-p_{4}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{5}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{p_{6}})\zeta_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}-p_{5}-p_{6}})\rangle\rangle\Bigg].

Performing Wick contraction on the ten-point function, there are 9 distinct non-zero contractions and we name them as the ’loops’ term, the ’
F^{2}G^{2}(1)
’ term, the ’
F^{2}G^{2}(2)
’ term, the ’
F^{2}G^{2}(3)
’ term, the ’
F^{2}G^{2}(4)
’ term, the ’
F^{2}G^{2}(5)
’ term, the ’
F^{2}G^{2}(6)
’ term, the ’
F^{2}G^{2}(7)
’ term and the ’8
F^{2}G^{2}(8)
’ term. Then we have

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k)=

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{loops}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(1)}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(2)}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(3)}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(4)}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(5)}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(6)}(k)

(102)

\displaystyle+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(7)}(k)+\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(8)}(k),

and we now demonstrate each of these 9 parts in detail.

The ’loops’ term can be easily obtained as

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{loops}(k)=

\displaystyle 9G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{1}\mathrm{d}p_{2}}{p_{1}p_{2}}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{1})\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{2})\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k)+3G_{\mathrm{NL}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{1}}{p_{1}}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{1})\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}}(k)-6G_{\mathrm{NL}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{2}}{p_{2}}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{2})\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3G_{\mathrm{NL}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{1}}{p_{1}}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{1})\left(\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}}(k)-3G_{\mathrm{NL}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}p_{2}}{p_{2}}\mathcal{P}_{g}(p_{2})\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k)\right),

(103)

and for power spectrum satisfying Eq. (
51
), we have
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{loops}(k)=3AG_{\mathrm{NL}}\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}}(k)-9A^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}(k)
.

F^{2}G^{2}(1)
’ term is a disconnected diagram and one example of the contraction is shown as follows:

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{}{{}}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{
{}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}}
{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 10.3 L 0 31.13 L 0 31.13 L 0 10.3}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}_{g}(\bm{k^{\prime}-q^{\prime}-p_{5}-p_{6}})\vbox to25.00002pt{}{}\right\rangle.

(104)

The symmetry factor in this case is 12. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(1)}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q}{(2\pi)^{3}}I^{2}\left(u,v\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{3}}{(2\pi)^{9}}4P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{3}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q-p_{2}-p_{3}}|\right)\times 12

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{3}\int_{|1-u_{3}|}^{1+u_{3}}\mathrm{d}v_{3}I^{2}(u,v)

(105)

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{u^{2}v^{2}u_{1}^{2}v_{1}^{2}u_{2}^{2}v_{2}^{2}u_{3}^{2}v_{3}^{2}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v_{1}uk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}vk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{3}v_{2}vk\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(v_{3}v_{2}vk\right),

where the second equality in the above equation is obtained by performing the coordinate transformation
u_{1}=p_{1}/q
,
v_{1}=|\bm{q-p_{1}}|/q
,
u_{2}=p_{2}/|\bm{k-q}|
,
v_{2}=|\bm{k-q-p_{2}}|/|\bm{k-q}|
,
u_{3}=p_{3}/|\bm{k-q-p_{2}}|
,
v_{3}=|\bm{k-q-p_{2}-p_{3}}|/|\bm{k-q-p_{2}}|
.

(106)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 72. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(2)}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}

\displaystyle\times 4P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k+p_{1}+p_{2}-q}|\right)P_{g}\left(\bm{q+q^{\prime}-p_{1}}\right)\times 72

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{4\pi^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}

(107)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}}{(u_{1}u_{2}w_{13}w_{0134}w_{123})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{0134}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{123}k\right),

where
w_{13}
is defined in Eq. (
45
) and
w_{0134}
and
w_{123}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{0134}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k-q+p_{1}+p_{2}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+u^{2}+u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}-2u\cos\theta+2u_{1}\cos\theta_{1}+2u_{2}\cos\theta_{2}-2uu_{1}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1}\right]

(108)

\displaystyle-2uu_{2}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{2}\cos\varphi_{3}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{3}\right]+2u_{1}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right],

\displaystyle w_{123}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{q+q^{\prime}-p_{1}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u^{2}+u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}+2uu^{\prime}\left(\sin\theta\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\varphi_{1}+\cos\theta\cos\theta^{\prime}\right)-2uu_{1}(\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1})

(109)

\displaystyle-2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right].

and the symmetry factor in this case is 72. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(3)}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}

\displaystyle\times 4P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q-q^{\prime}+p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)\times 72

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{4\pi^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}

(111)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}}{(u_{1}u_{2}w_{13}w_{23}w_{01234})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{23}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{01234}k\right),

where
w_{13}
and
w_{23}
are defined in Eq. (
45
) and Eq. (
46
) and
w_{01234}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{01234}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k-q-q^{\prime}+p_{1}-p_{2}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+u^{2}+u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}-2u\cos\theta-2u^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime}+2u_{1}\cos\theta_{1}-2u_{2}\cos\theta_{2}+2uu^{\prime}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\varphi_{1}+\cos\theta\cos\theta^{\prime}\right]

(112)

\displaystyle-2uu_{1}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1}\right]+2uu_{2}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{2}\cos\varphi_{3}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{2}\right]

\displaystyle-2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right]+2u^{\prime}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{2}\right]

\displaystyle-2u_{1}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right].

and the symmetry factor in this case is 36. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(4)}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}

\displaystyle\times 4P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q-q^{\prime}}|\right)\times 36

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{8\pi^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}

(114)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}}{(u_{1}u_{2}w_{13}w_{24}w_{012})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{24}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{012}k\right),

where
w_{13}
and
w_{012}
are defined in Eq. (
45
) and Eq. (
32
) and
w_{24}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{24}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{2}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}=u^{\prime 2}+u_{2}^{2}-2u^{\prime}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{2}\right].

(115)

(116)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 144. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(5)}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}

\displaystyle\times 2P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q+p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q+q^{\prime}-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)\times 144

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{4\pi^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}

(117)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}}{(u_{1}u_{2}w_{13}w_{013}w_{1234})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{013}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{1234}k\right),

where
w_{013}
and
w_{1234}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{013}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k-q+p_{1}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}=1+u^{2}+u_{1}^{2}-2u\cos\theta+2u_{1}\cos\theta_{1}-2uu_{1}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1}\right],

(118)

\displaystyle w_{1234}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{q+q^{\prime}-p_{1}-p_{2}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u^{2}+u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}+2uu^{\prime}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta^{\prime}\cos\varphi_{1}+\cos\theta\cos\theta^{\prime}\right]-2uu_{1}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{1}\cos\varphi_{2}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{1}\right]

(119)

\displaystyle-2uu_{2}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{2}\cos\varphi_{3}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{2}\right]-2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right]

\displaystyle-2u^{\prime}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{2}\right]+2u_{1}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right].

(120)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 72. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(6)}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}

\displaystyle\times 2P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-q^{\prime}}|\right)\times 72

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{8\pi^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}

(121)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}}{(u_{1}u_{2}w_{13}w_{014}w_{12})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{014}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{12}k\right),

where
w_{014}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{014}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k-q-p_{2}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}=1+u^{2}+u_{2}^{2}-2u\cos\theta-2u_{2}\cos\theta_{2}+2uu_{2}\left[\sin\theta\sin\theta_{2}\cos\varphi_{3}+\cos\theta\cos\theta_{2}\right].

(122)

(123)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 144. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(7)}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}

\displaystyle\times 2P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-q-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)\times 144

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{4\pi^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}

(124)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}}{(u_{1}u_{2}w_{13}w_{014}w_{234})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{014}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{234}k\right),

where
w_{234}
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{234}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{q^{\prime}-p_{1}-p_{2}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}-2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right]-2u^{\prime}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{2}\right]

(125)

\displaystyle+2u_{1}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right].

(126)

and the symmetry factor in this case is 144. Expanding the correlation function and using the appearing delta functions to eliminate redundant integrals, we can obtain:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{F^{2}G^{2}(8)}(k)=

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}k^{3}}{6\pi^{2}}\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}q\mathrm{d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{6}}\cos 2(\phi-\phi^{\prime})I\left(u,v\right)I\left(u^{\prime},v^{\prime}\right)\int\frac{\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{1}\mathrm{d}^{3}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{6}}

\displaystyle\times 4P_{g}\left(p_{1}\right)P_{g}\left(p_{2}\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{q-p_{1}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)P_{g}\left(|\bm{k+q^{\prime}-p_{1}-p_{2}}|\right)\times 144

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{3F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}}{2\pi^{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u^{\prime}\int_{|1-u^{\prime}|}^{1+u^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}

(127)

\displaystyle\times\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}\cos 2\varphi_{1}I(u,v)I(u^{\prime},v^{\prime})\frac{uvu^{\prime}v^{\prime}u_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}}{(u_{1}u_{2}w_{13}w_{034}w_{0234})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{13}k\right)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{034}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{0234}k\right),

where
w_{13}
is defined in Eq. (
45
) and
w_{034}
and
w_{0234}
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle w_{034}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k-p_{1}-p_{2}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}-2u_{1}\cos\theta_{1}-2u_{2}\cos\theta_{2}+2u_{1}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right],

(128)

\displaystyle w_{0234}^{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{|\bm{k+q^{\prime}-p_{1}-p_{2}}|^{2}}{k^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+u^{\prime 2}+u_{1}^{2}+u_{2}^{2}+2u^{\prime}\cos\theta^{\prime}-2u_{1}\cos\theta_{1}-2u_{2}\cos\theta_{2}-2u^{\prime}u_{1}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{1}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{2})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{1}\right]

(129)

\displaystyle-2u^{\prime}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta^{\prime}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{1}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta^{\prime}\cos\theta_{3}\right]+2u_{1}u_{2}\left[\sin\theta_{1}\sin\theta_{2}\cos(\varphi_{2}-\varphi_{3})+\cos\theta_{1}\cos\theta_{2}\right].

IV
Log-dependent behavior in the infrared region

In this section, we will demonstrate that all the non-Gaussian diagrams have a similar scaling in the infrared region, characterized by the following logarithmic dependence:

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}\propto\left(\frac{k}{k_{\star}}\right)^{3}\ln^{2}\left(\frac{4k_{\star}^{2}}{3k^{2}}\right),

(130)

where
k_{\star}
is a reference scale which we will discuss below and the slope index is given by:

n_{\mathrm{GW}}\equiv\frac{\mathrm{d}\ln\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}}{\mathrm{d}\ln k}=3-\frac{4}{\ln\frac{4k_{\star}^{2}}{3k^{2}}}.

(131)

This logarithmic scaling law was initially investigated in
Yuan et al. 2020a
for the Gaussian case, where the authors considered a generic power spectrum with a peak at
k_{*}
. More recently, in
Adshead et al. 2021
, the authors also identified logarithmic scaling for
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}
terms and
F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}
terms. In this study, we provide a proof for the “tri” term as an example using the methodology outlined in
Yuan et al. 2020a
.
First of all, we rewrite the “tri” term as follows:

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)=

\displaystyle{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\over 4\pi^{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}

(132)

\displaystyle\times I^{2}(u,v)\frac{uvu_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}}{(u_{1}u_{2}w_{134}v)^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{134}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(uk\right).

To effectively analyze the scaling, we consider a generic power spectrum with a peak at
k_{*}
and introduce two parameters,
k_{-}
and
k_{+}
, in such a way that the power spectrum is mainly distributed in
k\in[k_{-},k_{+}]
and we neglect the portion beyond this range. Since the integral involves terms of the form
\mathcal{P}_{g}(u_{1}k)\mathcal{P}_{g}(u_{2}k)\mathcal{P}_{g}(uk)
, it follows that
u_{1}k
,
u_{2}k
, and
uk
are constrained within the range
[k_{-},k_{+}]
. Consequently, this imposes lower and upper limits on the variables
u_{1}
,
u_{2}
, and
u
, namely

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)=

\displaystyle{G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\over 4\pi^{2}}\int_{k_{-}/k}^{k_{+}/k}\mathrm{d}u\int_{|1-u|}^{1+u}\mathrm{d}v\int_{k_{-}/k}^{k_{+}/k}\mathrm{d}u_{1}\int_{|1-u_{1}|}^{1+u_{1}}\mathrm{d}v_{1}\int_{k_{-}/k}^{k_{+}/k}\mathrm{d}u_{2}\int_{|1-u_{2}|}^{1+u_{2}}\mathrm{d}v_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathrm{d}\varphi_{3}

(133)

\displaystyle\times I^{2}(u,v)\frac{uvu_{1}v_{1}u_{2}v_{2}}{(u_{1}u_{2}w_{134}v)^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{0134}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(uk\right).

Since we are interested in the infrared region where
k\ll k_{*}
, it follows that
u
,
u_{1}
, and
u_{2}
are much greater than
1
. Consequently, we can simplify the above equation employing the first mean value theorem for definite integrals

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)=

\displaystyle{2G_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}\over\pi^{2}}\left({k_{+}-k_{-}\over k}\right)^{3}I^{2}(u^{*},v^{*})\frac{u^{*}v^{*}u_{1}^{*}v_{1}^{*}u_{2}^{*}v_{2}^{*}}{(u_{1}^{*}u_{2}^{*}w_{134}^{*}v^{*})^{3}}\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{1}^{*}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u_{2}^{*}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(w_{0134}^{*}k\right)\mathcal{P}_{g}\left(u^{*}k\right),

(134)

where
u^{*},u_{1}^{*},u_{2}^{*}\in[k_{-}/k,k_{+}/k]
and
v^{*},v_{1}^{*},v_{2}^{*}
are in the range of
[u^{*}-1,u^{*}+1]
,
[u_{1}^{*}-1,u_{1}^{*}+1]
and
[u_{2}^{*}-1,u_{2}^{*}+1]
respectively.
w_{0134}^{*}
is defined as replacing
u,v,u_{1},v_{1},u_{2},v_{2},\varphi_{2},\varphi_{3}
in Eq. (
III.7
) with
u^{*},v^{*},u_{1}^{*},v_{1}^{*},u_{2}^{*},v_{2}^{*},\varphi_{2}^{*},\varphi_{3}^{*}
and we have
\varphi_{2}^{*},\varphi_{3}^{*}\in[0,2\pi]
. By expanding
u^{*},u_{1}^{*},u_{2}^{*}
at
k_{\star}/k
to leading order where
k_{\star}\in[k_{-},k_{+}]
is a reference scale, we obtain

\displaystyle\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}^{tri}(k)\propto\left({k\over k_{\star}}\right)^{3}I^{2}\left({k_{\star}\over k},{k_{\star}\over k}\right).

(135)

Using the following asymptotic behavior for
u\gg 1
:

I^{2}(u,u)\simeq{9\over 4}\ln^{2}\left({4u^{2}\over 3}\right),

(136)

we finally get Eq. (
130
). All the scaling of non-Gaussian diagrams in the infrared region can be shown in the similar way.

It has been argued in
Yuan et al. 2020a
that this log-dependent scaling could be smoking gun for SIGW. However, the mean value theorm could not give us the exact value of the reference scale,
k_{\star}
, and one should treat
k_{\star}
as free parameter in GW data analysis.
Moreover, the value of
k_{\star}
is different for different power spectrum and different non-Gaussian diagrams. Next, we will show that the scaling of the total energy spectrum also follows Eq. (
130
).
First of all, we write down the total energy spectrum in the infrared region in a generic form as follows:

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k)=\sum_{i}A_{i}\left(\frac{k}{k_{\star i}}\right)^{3}\ln^{2}\left({4k_{\star i}^{2}\over 3k^{2}}\right),

(137)

where
A_{i}
denotes the amplitude of the
i
-th non-Gaussian energy spectrum and
k_{\star i}
is the reference scale obtained using the mean value theorem for the
i
-th non-Gaussian energy spectrum. The above equation can be re-written as

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k)=k^{3}\sum_{i}{A_{i}\over k_{\star i}^{3}}\left(c_{i}^{2}+2c_{i}\ln\left({4k_{\star}^{2}\over 3k^{2}}\right)+\ln^{2}\left({4k_{\star}^{2}\over 3k^{2}}\right)\right),

(138)

where we introduce
c_{i}\equiv\ln\left({k_{\star i}^{2}\over k_{\star}^{2}}\right)
and
k_{\star}
is a reference scale. Note that
k_{\star i}
is obtained using the mean value theorem and it is in the range of
[k_{-},k_{+}]
. On the other hand, we choose
k_{\star}
also to be in the range of
[k_{-},k_{+}]
. Then we have

{k_{-}\over k_{+}}\lesssim{k_{\star}\over k_{\star i}}\lesssim{k_{+}\over k_{-}}

(139)

In the infrared region where
k\ll k_{-}
, we obtain the relation

c_{i}\ll\ln\left({4k_{\star}^{2}\over 3k^{2}}\right),\mathrm{if}{k\over k_{\star}}\ll{k_{-}\over k_{+}}

(140)

This indicates that, in the infrared region where
k/k_{\star}\ll k_{-}/k_{+}
, the
c_{i}
terms in Eq. (
138
) are negligible and we can obtain

\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}(k)\simeq k^{3}\ln^{2}\left({4k_{\star}^{2}\over 3k^{2}}\right)\sum_{i}{A_{i}\over k_{\star i}^{3}},

(141)

This finally lead us to Eq. (
130
) and the slope index is still given by Eq. (
131
).
Note that the region where
k/k_{\star}\ll k_{-}/k_{+}
depends on the width of the power spectrum through
k_{-}/k_{+}
. For narrow spectrum, it reduces to
k_{-}/k_{+}\simeq 1
. While for some wide spectrum where
k_{+}
is several orders of magnitude larger than
k_{-}
, Eq. (
131
) is a approximation only for sufficient small
k
.

Our results show that, the SIGWs from PBH formation will also exhibit a log-dependent scaling in the infrared region, regardless of the specific shape of the power spectrum even in the non-Gaussian case. This log-dependent scaling comes from the oscillating behavior of the evolution of the scalar perturbations during RD, and could be a smoking gun for detecting SIGW from PBHs.

To have deeper insights into the influence of non-Gaussianities on the energy spectrum SIGWs, we consider a log-normal shape spectrum which is widely used when studying the SIGW spectrum (e.g.,
Inomata and Nakama 2019
;
Pi and Sasaki 2020
;
Yuan and Huang 2021a
;
Kohri and Terada 2021
;
Meng et al. 2022
;
Yuan and Huang 2021b
), namely

\mathcal{P}_{g}(k)=\frac{A}{\sqrt{2\pi\sigma_{*}^{2}}}\exp\left(-\frac{\ln^{2}\left(k/k_{*}\right)}{2\sigma_{*}^{2}}\right),

(142)

where the dimensionless parameter
\sigma_{*}
is related to the width of the spectrum and we normalize the power spectrum in such way that
\int\mathcal{P}_{g}(k)d\ln k=A
.

Given that analytical results for the non-Gaussian diagrams are challenging to obtain, in this regard, we use the
Cuba.jl
package
Hahn 2005
;
Hahn 2015
to present the numerical results. Fig.
1
illustrates the energy spectrum of SIGW for each non-Gaussian term. As anticipated, the spectra exhibit peaks centered around
k_{*}
. Furthermore, the spectra sharply decrease for
k/k_{*}\gtrsim 2
, with the non-Gaussian terms displaying larger drop-off wavelength compared to the Gaussian term due to momentum conservation. Moreover, the non-Gaussian energy spectrum demonstrate a log-dependent scaling described by Eq. (
130
) in the infrared region.

Figure 1:
The unscaled (By setting
A=1
,
F_{\mathrm{NL}}=1
and
G_{\mathrm{NL}}=1
) energy spectrum of SIGW generated by a log-normal power spectrum described by Eq. (
142
) with
\sigma_{*}=0.2
.

In Fig.
2
, we present the total energy spectrum of SIGW by today for some representative values of
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
,
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
A
.

Figure 2:
The total energy spectrum of SIGW generated by a log-normal power spectrum described by Eq. (
142
) with
\sigma_{*}=0.2
. The brown line in each panel denotes the power-law sensitivity curve of LISA, assuming a
4
year detection time. We set
A=10^{-3}
for both panels. Left panel: The energy spectrum in the absence of
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
. Right panel: The energy spectrum in the absence of
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
.

As shown in Fig.
2
, the role of non-Gaussian corrections might change the amplitude and the shape of the energy spectrum. The shape of the energy spectrum is modified mainly around
k_{*}
but in the infrared region it exhibits a log-dependent scaling given by Eq. (
131
).
It is worth mentioning that we treat
F_{\mathrm{NL}}
and
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
as free parameters in this paper. Nevertheless, within certain inflationary models, a perturbativity condition could be applicable to the non-Gaussian parameters
Kristiano and Yokoyama 2022a
;
Meng et al. 2022
;
Kristiano and Yokoyama 2022b
;
Garcia-Saenz et al. 2023
. Consequently, the non-Gaussian parameters may not retain.

V
Conclusion and Discussion

In this paper, we study the full impacts of a local-type non-Gaussianities up to
G_{\mathrm{NL}}
order on SIGW and derive semi-analytical results for arbitrary primordial power spectrum. All the non-Gaussian terms to the energy spectrum of SIGW exhibit a log-dependent scaling in the infrared region. This log-dependent scaling distinguishes SIGW from other GW energy spectra generated by currently known physical processes, making it a smoking gun for detecting the SIGW. Recently, NANOGrav collaboration searched the SIGW data for various power spectrum and claimed that the NANOGrav 15-year data is well fit in the low-frequency tail of SIGW
Afzal et al. 2023
, indicating the significance of the log-dependent scaling in searching the SIGW signals.

In addition to expanding the curvature perturbation in a non-Gaussian manner, it is feasible to extend the expansion of the source term in the Einstein equations to higher orders, thereby obtaining higher-order corrections to
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}

Yuan et al. 2019
;
Chang et al. 2023
. For instance, considering the third-order tensor mode
h_{ij}^{(3)}
, the subsequent-order correction to
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}
(assuming
G_{\mathrm{NL}}=0
for illustration) would arise from
\left\langle h^{(2)}h^{(3)}\right\rangle\sim F_{\mathrm{NL}}A_{g}^{3}+F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{3}A_{g}^{4}+F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{5}A_{g}^{5}
or
\left\langle h^{(3)}h^{(3)}\right\rangle\sim A_{g}^{3}+F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{2}A_{g}^{4}+F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{4}A_{g}^{5}+F_{\mathrm{NL}}^{6}A_{g}^{6}
. However, in scenarios involving the formation of PBHs within non-Gaussian regimes, both
F_{\mathrm{NL}}A
and
A_{g}
are much smaller than
1
when the abundance of PBHs in the Universe is fixed (refer to e.g., Fig. 6 in
Cai et al. 2019c
). Consequently, this leads to the inference that corrections from
\left\langle h^{(2)}h^{(3)}\right\rangle
and
\left\langle h^{(3)}h^{(3)}\right\rangle
are at least an order of magnitude smaller, by a factor of
F_{\mathrm{NL}}A_{g}
and
A_{g}
respectively, when compared to the leading order of
\Omega_{\mathrm{GW}}
. This conclusion implies that even upon considering the non-Gaussian expansion of higher order source terms, the resultant corrections remain subdominant.
